    # CC-NEWS WARC → PostgreSQL (Spark)
    
    Streams Common Crawl News (CC-NEWS) WARC files, extracts article text, detects
    language (ar/en/fr), and loads results into PostgreSQL. Heavy WARC
    download + parse + HTML extraction runs on Spark workers; language filtering
    and DB writes run on the driver.
    
    ## Layout
    
    | File | Purpose |
    |---|---|
    | `stream_to_db.py` | Main job: WARC → DB. Use this for real loads. |
    | `run_to_db.sh` | `spark-submit --master yarn` wrapper for `stream_to_db.py`. |
    | `parsers/parsers.py` | Streaming WARC parser: `warcio` record-at-a-time, no staging. |
    | `extractors/extractors.py` | HTML extraction (**trafilatura** main-content first, selectolax fallback), Lingua language ID (20 languages, no torch). |
    | `db/db_handler.py` | Postgres schema + batched inserts. |
    | `utils.py` | Encoding cleanup, Arabic validation, language gate. |
    | `db_inspect.py` | Prints table counts, checkpoint progress, and sample rows. |
    | `langchain.py` | Loads DB rows into LangChain `Document`s + text splitter (batched server-side cursor, creds from `db_handler.DB_CONFIG`). |
    | `pyspark_venv.tar.gz` | Packed worker venv shipped via `--archives` (rebuild if deps change). |
    
    ## How it runs (workers vs driver)
    
    `stream_to_db.py`:
    
    1. Driver fetches the `warc.paths.gz` manifest, takes the first `NUM_FILES` URLs
       (`NUM_FILES` unset/empty/all/0 = whole manifest).
    2. Rounds over the unfinished-file set (`sc.parallelize(active).mapPartitions(process_partition).toLocalIterator()`) —
       each worker **streams** its WARC file: `warcio` parses each
       article as its bytes arrive, runs `extract_html_fields`, yields fields only
       (`raw_bytes` is dropped before yield). Files with a byte checkpoint resume
       via HTTP `Range` from `bytes_done - RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES` (see Resume
       below); otherwise the stream starts at byte 0. Hitting `MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND`
       closes the connection early so each round transfers a bounded chunk; files
       reaching `MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE` accepted articles or EOF drop out of the
       next round's set.
    3. Driver iterates records, batches `DB_BATCH_SIZE` texts through
       `detect_languages_batch`, keeps only
       ar/en/fr (`is_target_language`), calls `save_batch_records`.
    
    So workers do network + CPU-heavy extraction; the driver does language ID
    and all Postgres I/O. `toLocalIterator` streams results — workers are not
    blocked on the driver, but the driver is the throughput bottleneck for large
    `NUM_FILES`. DB writes are single-writer from the driver (no concurrent
    worker writes).
    
    ## Prerequisites
    
    On this master, `pyspark` and `spark-submit` are on PATH. Both the `.venv/`
    environment and the environment at the project root can import the existing
    Spark 4.2.0 installation through `spark_cluster.pth` in their site-packages.
    The system Python also has this connection in the `spark` user's site-packages.
    These files point to `/opt/spark/python` and its matching bundled Py4J library;
    there is no separate PySpark version to keep in sync. VS Code defaults to `.venv`.
    
    ```bash
    cd /home/spark/spark_warc
    source .venv/bin/activate
    python -c 'import pyspark; print(pyspark.__version__)'
    ```
    
    The job inherits the cluster's three-executor memory settings unless overridden
    at submission. Use `./run_to_db.sh` for distributed crawling so the project code
    and Python dependencies are shipped to the workers. Install the requirements
    below before running the full application.
    
    - Hadoop/YARN + Spark 4.2 (`spark-submit` on PATH, `SPARK_HOME=/opt/spark`).
    - Python venv at `.venv/` with `requirements.txt` installed.
    - `venv-pack` and `zip` CLIs (used by the run scripts).
    - PostgreSQL reachable from the driver host (default `localhost:5432`).
    
    ## Configuration
    
    ### Database — `db/db_handler.py:6-12`
    
    ```python
    DB_CONFIG = {"dbname": "warcdb", "user": "warc_user",
                 "password": "password", "host": "localhost", "port": "5432"}
    ```
    
    `init_db()` creates `websites`, `authors`, `pages`, `metadata`, `content`,
    `ingest_files` if missing. Tables are joined as
    `pages → websites/authors`, `metadata.page_id → pages.id`,
    `content.page_id → pages.id`. `warc_record_id` is unique; re-runs upsert.
    `ingest_files` holds byte-range checkpoints per WARC file (see Resume).
    
    ### Job tunables — `stream_to_db.py:18-24`
    
    | Setting | Default | Override |
    |---|---|---|
    | `YEAR` / `MONTH` | `2026` / `05` | edit file |
    | `NUM_FILES` | `15` | files from `START_FILE_OFFSET`; unset/empty/all/0 = to end of manifest |
    | `START_FILE_OFFSET` | `0` | skip the first N manifest entries (file-range targeting) |
    | `MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND` | 2000 | per-round transfer chunk; `0` = unlimited; counts *extracted* articles (pre-filter, worker-visible), closes the connection early each round |
    | `MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE` | `0` (file to EOF) | per-file stop on *accepted* articles (target-language + fresh, driver-side); the driver re-submits the file from its byte checkpoint until this many are accepted or EOF |
    | `MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND` | `6` | in-flight files per round (bounds task-result blocks for any manifest size); `0` = all unfinished files each round |
    | `MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES` | `0` (unlimited) | global stop on *accepted* articles (target-language + fresh); run stops + cancels Spark tasks when reached |
    | `NO_PROGRESS` | `0` | `NO_PROGRESS=1` disables the stderr progress bar |
    | `RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES` | `1048576` (1 MiB) | rewind before stored offset on resume; overlap dups collapse via `record_id`; `0` = resume exactly at stored bytes |
    | `USE_HDFS_CACHE` | `1` | `0` disables both shared HDFS artifacts (worker venv + Spark jars), falling back to per-run uploads |
    | `MAX_RETRIES` | `3` | download + DB retry budget |
    | `MIN_WORD_COUNT` | `80` | article word minimum (trafilatura acceptance + yield gate) |
    | `DB_BATCH_SIZE` | `25` | edit file or env |
    
    Renamed in the 2026-09-29 cleanup (old names removed, no fallback):
    
    | Old | New |
    |---|---|
    | `MAX_ARTICLES_PER_FILE` | `MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND` |
    | `MAX_TOTAL_ARTICLES` | `MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES` (note: counts *accepted*, old counted extracted) |
    | `USE_HDFS_VENV` / `USE_SPARK_ARCHIVE` | `USE_HDFS_CACHE` (single flag for both) |
    | `DOWNLOAD_RETRIES` / `DB_RETRIES` | `MAX_RETRIES` |
    | `USE_TRAFILATURA` (`extractors`) | `"1"` | `USE_TRAFILATURA=0` → legacy selectolax-only extraction |
    | `SPARK_MASTER_URL` | unset (inherit from `spark-submit`) | set only to force, e.g. `local[*]` for local test |
    
    Spark driver/executor memory (`2g` each, 6 executors) is set in
    `stream_to_db.py:get_spark_session`. Language detection runs on the driver,
    so raising driver memory helps more than executor memory for large batches.
    Spark log level is `WARN` on the driver; a `tqdm` progress bar on stderr
    tracks accepted/target when capped, extracted count when uncapped, plus
    files/extracted/rejected/skipped/saved
    (`NO_PROGRESS=1` disables it, final summary print is unchanged).
    Task results stay direct up to 32 MB (`spark.task.maxDirectResultSize`):
    larger results would need a new driver→executor connection, which this
    cluster refuses — keep `MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND` modest (≤~200) so chunks
    stay well under that budget.
    
    ### Language ID (Lingua, no torch)
    
    `detect_languages_batch` uses `lingua-language-detector` (n-gram models,
    20 languages: ar/en/fr + es/de/it/pt/ca/ro/ru/tr/vi/ja/ko/zh/nl/ta/uk + fa/ur),
    built lazily once per driver process. Top confidence below
    `LINGUA_MIN_CONFIDENCE=0.5` → `other` (rejected by `is_target_language`).
    Short (<20 chars) → `unknown`, script-less → `ignored`. Accepted codes
    (`ar`/`en`/`fr`) additionally require ≤10% of the full text's alpha chars
    outside Latin/Arabic/Cyrillic/CJK — boilerplate chrome (English nav/footer)
    can't smuggle a Kannada/Hindi/Telugu body past the gate on volume.
    Arabic hits are re-validated with `is_valid_arabic_text` (→ `corrupted` on failure), which
    also rejects Arabic-script neighbours Lingua lacks (Kurdish Sorani) or that
    still score `ar` (Persian/Urdu stragglers) via distinctive letters
    (پچژگک ی / ٹڈڑںھے / ەڕڵۆێ). Arabic dialect is always stored as `MSA`
    (no dialect model).
    
    ### Article text extraction (trafilatura)
    
    `extract_html_fields` tries **trafilatura** main-content extraction first
    (`include_comments=False, include_tables=False`), accepted when ≥80 words;
    otherwise it falls back to the legacy selectolax body text. Anything still
    under 80 words is dropped. Each returned record carries
    `extraction: trafilatura|selectolax|...`, and the driver's summary prints the
    split (`via trafilatura` / `via fallback`).
    
    Drop test (200 real CC-NEWS articles, head-to-head, measured 2026-09-24):
    trafilatura-first kept **78.5%** vs old-method-alone 66.5% (**+12pp** — it
    salvages pages whose boilerplate-heavy body falls under the word gate),
    median 443 vs 977 words (~55% boilerplate stripped). Spot checks confirm clean
    article prose including non-Latin scripts. No extra drops: the fallback chain
    is a superset of the old behavior. Note trafilatura is GPL-3.0 (fine for
    internal use).
    
    ## Running
    
    ### 1. Full DB load on the cluster (normal path)
    
    ```bash
    cd /home/spark-master/rag_news_project/spark_warc_project
    ./run_to_db.sh                  # NUM_FILES=15, 2000 articles/file max
    ```
    
    Get-all (whole manifest, no caps):
    
    ```bash
    NUM_FILES=all MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES=0 ./run_to_db.sh
    ```
    
    Capped run (stop after N accepted articles total):
    
    ```bash
    MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES=10000 ./run_to_db.sh
    ```
    
    ### Resume (per-article + byte-range checkpoints)
    Re-running the same command skips already-saved articles (`warc_record_id`
    existence check per batch + in-run set + upsert backstop). Verified:
    second identical run reported `Skipped (already saved): 45, Saved: 0`,
    and `pages` holds 45 rows with zero duplicate `warc_record_id`s.
    
    Beyond that, every run maintains **byte-range checkpoints** in the
    `ingest_files` table (`file_url` PK, `manifest_id`, `bytes_done`,
    `articles_done`, `complete`), namespaced by `YEAR/MONTH`:
    - Files marked `complete` (streamed to true EOF) are never opened again;
      the log reports them as `checkpoint-skipped`.
    - Partial files resume from `bytes_done - RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES` (default
      1 MiB) via HTTP `Range`, with a forward scan to the next gzip member.
      The ~1 MiB overlap re-yields a few dozen articles that collapse via
      `record_id`; the straddled boundary article is the accepted loss.
    - Checkpoints commit on every `DB_BATCH_SIZE` flush and on file-done
      (**flush-then-commit** order, so a crash only ever re-works one batch).
    - Fallbacks (all automatic, no flags): server ignores `Range` → parse all
      and skip extraction for the stored article count; `416` or resync miss
      → one full-GET retry; checkpoint DB unreachable → run proceeds
      un-checkpointed with a warning.
    
    So a repeat run costs ~zero for finished files and only the unseen tail
    for partial ones — no more re-downloading file 1 every time.
    
    ### Retries / dead-letter
    - File download/parse: 3 attempts with backoff on transient errors
      (5xx/429/timeout); 4xx skips immediately; per-record errors skip the record.
    - DB batch: 3 attempts with rollback + reconnect; persistent failures go to
      `dead_letter/failed-batch-<ts>.jsonl` and the run continues.
    
    What the script does: activates `.venv`, rebuilds `pyspark_venv.tar.gz` only
    if missing, reuses/uploads a content-addressed copy on HDFS
    (`/user/spark-master/apps/spark/pyspark_venv-<md5>.tar.gz` — one upload per
    venv build, old builds pruned to newest 2; falls back to per-run upload if
    HDFS is unreachable), zips `extractors parsers db utils.py` into `--py-files`,
    sets driver python to `.venv` and worker python to `./environment/bin/python`,
    then `spark-submit --master yarn --deploy-mode client stream_to_db.py`.
    `USE_HDFS_CACHE=1` (default) shares the worker venv and Spark jars via
    HDFS instead of per-run uploads
    (disable per-run with `USE_HDFS_CACHE=0`).
    
    ## Cluster ops (learned the hard way)
    
    - **NM disks fill up** → nodes go `UNHEALTHY` (`local-dirs usable space below
      90%`) and the queue stalls with *"cluster resource is empty"*. Check
      `yarn node -list`; culprits are NM localizer caches (one venv copy per app
      version — the HDFS scheme above shrinks this to one per venv build) and
      `userlogs`. On each worker (as `hadoop`):
      `df -h /data/hadoop/tmp <log-dir>`; safe to clear with no apps running:
      `rm -rf /data/hadoop/tmp/nm-local-dir/usercache/*` and old `userlogs/*`.
      Worker-side `yarn-site.xml` mitigations: lower
      `yarn.nodemanager.localizer.cache.cleanup.interval-ms` and
      `yarn.nodemanager.localizer.cache.target-size-mb`.
    - **HDFS safe mode**: if DataNodes die, the NameNode holds safe mode and all
      staging/uploads fail. Restart DNs (`hdfs --daemon start datanode` per worker),
      then `hdfs dfsadmin -safemode wait` on the master.
    - Orphaned `ACCEPTED` apps from killed drivers block the queue:
      `yarn application -list` + `yarn application -kill <id>`.

## Activity

    $ cat parsers/parsers.py
    import io
    from typing import Generator, Dict, Any, Optional
    
    
    def _readable_stream(value):
        if isinstance(value, (bytes, bytearray)):
            return io.BytesIO(value)
        if hasattr(value, "read"):
            return value
        raise TypeError(f"Unsupported WARC input type: {type(value)!r}")
    
    
    def parse_warc_records_streaming(stream) -> Generator[Dict[str, Any], None, None]:
        """Record-at-a-time streaming WARC parser.
    
        Parses each response record as its bytes arrive over a non-seekable
        stream (e.g. an open HTTP response). Holds O(one article) in memory and
        O(1) total — the file is never staged to disk or RAM. A truncated tail
        (connection close, early abort) ends iteration silently; complete records
        yielded so far are unaffected.
        """
        from warcio.archiveiterator import ArchiveIterator
    
        stream = _readable_stream(stream)
        try:
            iterator = ArchiveIterator(stream, arc2warc=True)
            for record in iterator:
                try:
                    if record.rec_type != "response":
                        continue
                    content_type = ""
                    if record.http_headers:
                        try:
                            content_type = record.http_headers.get_header("Content-Type") or ""
                        except Exception:
                            content_type = ""
                    if "text/html" not in content_type.lower():
                        continue
                    try:
                        raw_bytes = record.content_stream().read()
                    except Exception:
                        continue
                    if not raw_bytes:
                        continue
                    headers = record.rec_headers
                    yield {
                        "url": headers.get_header("WARC-Target-URI"),
                        "warc_date": headers.get_header("WARC-Date"),
                        "content_type": content_type,
                        "record_id": headers.get_header("WARC-Record-ID"),
                        "raw_bytes": raw_bytes,
                    }
                except Exception:
                    continue
        except Exception:
            # Truncated stream tail (early close, cut connection): stop iteration,
            # keep the complete records already yielded.
            return
    
    
    def parse_warc_stream_fastwarc(stream, max_warc_bytes: Optional[int] = None, **kwargs) -> Generator[Dict[str, Any], None, None]:
        """Streaming entry point (name kept for backwards compatibility).
    
        Uses the warcio record-at-a-time path: no byte cap, no temp file.
        Extra args (e.g. legacy max_warc_bytes) are accepted and ignored.
        """
        _ = max_warc_bytes
        _ = kwargs
        yield from parse_warc_records_streaming(stream)

    import sys
    import os
    import time
    import json
    import hashlib
    import gzip
    import socket
    import urllib.request
    import urllib.error
    from datetime import datetime, timezone
    from pyspark.sql import SparkSession
    
    BASE_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    if BASE_DIR not in sys.path:
        sys.path.insert(0, BASE_DIR)
    
    from extractors.extractors import extract_html_fields, detect_languages_batch
    from db.db_handler import (
        init_db, save_batch_records, get_connection,
        load_ingest_progress, upsert_ingest_progress,
    )
    from parsers.parsers import parse_warc_stream_fastwarc
    from utils import is_target_language
    
    YEAR = os.environ.get("YEAR", "2026")
    MONTH = os.environ.get("MONTH", "05")
    MANIFEST_ID = f"{YEAR}/{MONTH}"
    # Resume overlap: rewind this far before the stored byte offset so the
    # resync scan lands on a valid gzip member. Overlap duplicates collapse
    # driver-side via record_id (upsert-safe); the straddled boundary article
    # is the accepted loss. 0 disables byte resume (full GET every file).
    RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES", str(1024 * 1024)))
    GZIP_MAGIC = b"\x1f\x8b"
    
    
    class _CountingReader:
        """File-like byte counter for worker-side resume offsets.
    
        Wraps the HTTP response; warcio reads through it while every byte is
        counted. A pushback buffer lets the gzip resync scan return the member
        header it stopped at.
        """
    
        def __init__(self, raw):
            self._raw = raw
            self._buf = b""
            self.bytes_read = 0
    
        def read(self, size=-1):
            if size is None or size < 0:
                chunks = [self._buf]
                self._buf = b""
                while True:
                    data = self._raw.read(65536)
                    if not data:
                        break
                    chunks.append(data)
                out = b"".join(chunks)
            else:
                while len(self._buf) < size:
                    data = self._raw.read(max(65536, size - len(self._buf)))
                    if not data:
                        break
                    self._buf += data
                out, self._buf = self._buf[:size], self._buf[size:]
            self.bytes_read += len(out)
            return out
    
        def pushback(self, data: bytes) -> None:
            self._buf = data + self._buf
            self.bytes_read -= len(data)
    
        def __getattr__(self, name):
            # Delegate anything else (close, seekable, ...) to the raw stream
            # so warcio sees the file-like it expects.
            return getattr(self.__dict__["_raw"], name)
    
    
    def _resync_gzip_member(reader: _CountingReader, max_scan: int = 8 * 1024 * 1024) -> bool:
        """Advance a Range-resumed stream to the next gzip member boundary.
    
        Returns True once the member header is pushed back for the parser.
        False when no valid boundary appears within max_scan (caller falls
        back to a full GET). Header check is magic + CM==deflate + reserved
        FLG bits clear; false positives are ~1e-8 per position.
        """
        window = b""
        scanned = 0
        while scanned < max_scan:
            chunk = reader.read(65536)
            if not chunk:
                return False
            window += chunk
            scanned += len(chunk)
            pos = 0
            while True:
                idx = window.find(GZIP_MAGIC, pos)
                if idx == -1:
                    window = window[-1:]  # keep tail split-magic bytes
                    break
                if idx + 4 > len(window):
                    window = window[idx:]  # need more bytes to validate
                    pos = 0
                    break
                cm, flg = window[idx + 2], window[idx + 3]
                if cm == 8 and (flg & 0xE0) == 0:
                    reader.pushback(window[idx:])
                    return True
                pos = idx + 1
        return False
    
    
    PATHS_URL = f"https://data.commoncrawl.org/crawl-data/CC-NEWS/{YEAR}/{MONTH}/warc.paths.gz"
    HEADERS = {"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64)"}
    DB_BATCH_SIZE = int(os.environ.get("DB_BATCH_SIZE", "25"))
    
    MAX_RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("MAX_RETRIES", "3"))
    DEAD_LETTER_DIR = os.environ.get("DEAD_LETTER_DIR", os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "dead_letter"))
    
    # Article caps. MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND <= 0 means unlimited transfer per
    # round (worker-visible extracted count, the per-round transfer chunk).
    # MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES <= 0 means unlimited accepted articles for the run.
    # NUM_FILES None means to the end of the manifest ("end of the paths file").
    # START_FILE_OFFSET skips the first N manifest entries (file-range targeting).
    MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND = int(os.environ.get("MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND", "2000"))
    # Per-file accepted cap (target-language + fresh, counted driver-side).
    # The worker can't know acceptance (HF runs on the driver), so each file is
    # processed in rounds: every round resumes from the stored byte checkpoint
    # and the driver stops re-submitting the file once its accepted count hits
    # this cap (0 = unlimited, file runs to EOF).
    MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE = int(os.environ.get("MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE", "0"))
    # Files per round: bounds in-flight partitions/task-results no matter how big
    # the manifest is (0 = all unfinished files every round). Default matches the
    # executor count so each executor typically hosts one file-task.
    MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND = int(os.environ.get("MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND", "6"))
    # Article = MIN_WORD_COUNT+ words after extraction (both the trafilatura
    # acceptance and the yield gate; passed to workers via task closure).
    MIN_WORD_COUNT = int(os.environ.get("MIN_WORD_COUNT", "80"))
    MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES = int(os.environ.get("MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES", "0"))
    # Driver progress bar (stderr, silent when piped). NO_PROGRESS=1 disables it.
    USE_PROGRESS_BAR = os.environ.get("NO_PROGRESS", "0") != "1"
    
    
    def _parse_num_files(raw: str | None) -> int | None:
        if raw is None:
            return 15
        text = raw.strip().lower()
        if text in ("", "all", "none"):
            return None
        try:
            value = int(text)
        except ValueError:
            return 15
        return None if value <= 0 else value
    
    
    NUM_FILES = _parse_num_files(os.environ.get("NUM_FILES"))
    START_FILE_OFFSET = max(0, int(os.environ.get("START_FILE_OFFSET", "0") or 0))
    
    
    def get_spark_session() -> SparkSession:
        try:
            existing_session = SparkSession.getActiveSession()
            if existing_session:
                return existing_session
        except Exception:
            pass
    
        builder = (
            SparkSession.builder
            .appName("CC-NEWS-Stream-To-DB-Distributed-Cluster")
            # Inherit resource limits from /opt/spark/conf/spark-defaults.conf
            # or spark-submit; this cluster has three workers with 2 GB RAM each.
            .config("spark.sql.execution.arrow.pyspark.enabled", "true")
            # Task results under this size ride back on the existing
            # executor->driver channel (direct). Larger ones are stored in the
            # executor BlockManager and fetched over a NEW driver->executor
            # connection, which this cluster refuses: keep results direct.
            .config("spark.task.maxDirectResultSize", "32m")
        )
        # Only force a master when explicitly requested. Otherwise inherit it
        # from spark-submit (--master yarn) so the job actually uses workers.
        # Direct Python invocation also loads this installation's Spark defaults.
        if "SPARK_MASTER_URL" in os.environ:
            builder = builder.master(os.environ["SPARK_MASTER_URL"])
        return builder.getOrCreate()
    
    
    def make_record_id(record, url, text):
        rid = record.get("record_id", "")
        if rid:
            return rid
        basis = (url or "") + (text[:200] if text else "")
        return "gen-" + hashlib.md5(basis.encode("utf-8", errors="ignore")).hexdigest()
    
    
    def _is_transient_download_error(error: Exception) -> bool:
        if isinstance(error, (socket.timeout, TimeoutError, ConnectionError)):
            return True
        if isinstance(error, urllib.error.HTTPError):
            return error.code == 429 or 500 <= error.code < 600
        if isinstance(error, urllib.error.URLError):
            return True
        return False
    
    
    def _sleep_backoff(attempt: int) -> None:
        time.sleep(2 ** (attempt + 1))
    
    
    def _write_dead_letter(batch: list, error: Exception) -> str:
        os.makedirs(DEAD_LETTER_DIR, exist_ok=True)
        stamp = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ")
        path = os.path.join(DEAD_LETTER_DIR, f"failed-batch-{stamp}-{os.getpid()}.jsonl")
        payload = {"error": f"{type(error).__name__}: {error}", "records": batch}
        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            json.dump(payload, fh, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)
            fh.write("\n")
        return path
    
    
    def _filter_already_saved(conn, record_ids: list) -> set:
        """Return the subset of record_ids already present in pages."""
        if not record_ids:
            return set()
        cur = conn.cursor()
        try:
            cur.execute(
                "SELECT warc_record_id FROM pages WHERE warc_record_id = ANY(%s);",
                (record_ids,),
            )
            return {row[0] for row in cur.fetchall()}
        finally:
            cur.close()
    
    
    def _save_batch_with_retry(batch: list) -> tuple[int, str | None]:
        """Save one batch with reconnect + backoff. Returns (saved_count, dead_letter_path)."""
        last_error: Exception | None = None
        for attempt in range(MAX_RETRIES):
            conn = None
            try:
                conn = get_connection()
                saved_before = len(batch)
                save_batch_records(batch, conn=conn)
                return saved_before, None
            except Exception as error:
                last_error = error
                try:
                    if conn is not None:
                        conn.rollback()
                except Exception:
                    pass
                print(f"DB batch write failed (attempt {attempt + 1}/{MAX_RETRIES}): {error}")
                if attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
                    _sleep_backoff(attempt)
            finally:
                try:
                    if conn is not None:
                        conn.close()

    import psycopg2
    from psycopg2.extras import execute_values
    from urllib.parse import urlparse
    import json
    
    DB_CONFIG = {
        "dbname": "warcdb",
        "user": "warc_user",
        "password": "password",
        "host": "localhost",
        "port": "5432"
    }
    
    def get_connection():
        return psycopg2.connect(**DB_CONFIG)
    
    def init_db():
        create_tables_query = """
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS websites (
            id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
            domain VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
            created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS authors (
            id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
            name VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
            created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pages (
            id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
            website_id INT REFERENCES websites(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
            author_id INT REFERENCES authors(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
            warc_record_id VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE,
            url TEXT NOT NULL,
            created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS metadata (
            page_id INT PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES pages(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
            title TEXT,
            published_date VARCHAR(100),
            language VARCHAR(50),
            arabic_dialect VARCHAR(50),
            word_count INT,
            char_count INT,
            links_count INT,
            headings JSONB,
            created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS content (
            page_id INT PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES pages(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
            cleaned_text TEXT,
            created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ingest_files (
            file_url TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
            manifest_id VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
            bytes_done BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
            articles_done INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
            complete BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
            updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
        );
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_ingest_files_manifest ON ingest_files (manifest_id);
        """
        migration_query = """
        ALTER TABLE pages ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS author_id INT REFERENCES authors(id) ON DELETE SET NULL;
        ALTER TABLE metadata ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS arabic_dialect VARCHAR(50);
        """
        conn = get_connection()
        cur = conn.cursor()
        cur.execute(create_tables_query)
        cur.execute(migration_query)
        conn.commit()
        cur.close()
        conn.close()
    
    def extract_domain(url):
        try:
            parsed = urlparse(url)
            domain = parsed.netloc or parsed.path.split('/')[0]
            return domain.lower().replace('www.', '') if domain else "unknown_domain"
        except Exception:
            return "unknown_domain"
    
    def save_batch_records(records_list, conn=None):
        if not records_list:
            return
        own_connection = conn is None
        if own_connection:
            conn = get_connection()
        cur = conn.cursor()
        try:
            domains = list(set(extract_domain(r.get("url", "")) for r in records_list))
            execute_values(cur, "INSERT INTO websites (domain) VALUES %s ON CONFLICT (domain) DO NOTHING;", [(d,) for d in domains])
            cur.execute("SELECT domain, id FROM websites WHERE domain = ANY(%s);", (domains,))
            domain_map = dict(cur.fetchall())
    
            authors = list(set((r.get("author") or "").strip() for r in records_list if (r.get("author") or "").strip()))
            if authors:
                execute_values(cur, "INSERT INTO authors (name) VALUES %s ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING;", [(a,) for a in authors])
                cur.execute("SELECT name, id FROM authors WHERE name = ANY(%s);", (authors,))
                author_map = dict(cur.fetchall())
            else:
                author_map = {}
    
            pages_tuples = [
                (
                    domain_map.get(extract_domain(r.get("url", ""))),
                    author_map.get((r.get("author") or "").strip()),
                    r.get("record_id", ""),
                    r.get("url", "")
                )
                for r in records_list
            ]
            insert_pages_query = """
            INSERT INTO pages (website_id, author_id, warc_record_id, url)
            VALUES %s
            ON CONFLICT (warc_record_id) DO UPDATE SET url = EXCLUDED.url
            RETURNING warc_record_id, id;
            """
            inserted_pages = execute_values(cur, insert_pages_query, pages_tuples, fetch=True)
            page_id_map = {rec_id: p_id for rec_id, p_id in inserted_pages}
    
            metadata_tuples = []
            content_tuples = []
            for r in records_list:
                p_id = page_id_map.get(r.get("record_id", ""))
                if p_id:
                    metadata_tuples.append((
                        p_id, r.get("title", ""), r.get("published_date", ""),
                        r.get("language", "unknown"), r.get("arabic_dialect", "N/A"),
                        r.get("word_count", 0), r.get("char_count", 0),
                        r.get("links_count", 0), json.dumps(r.get("headings", []))
                    ))
                    content_tuples.append((p_id, r.get("cleaned_text", "")))
    
            if metadata_tuples:
                execute_values(cur, """INSERT INTO metadata (page_id, title, published_date, language, arabic_dialect, word_count, char_count, links_count, headings) VALUES %s ON CONFLICT (page_id) DO NOTHING;""", metadata_tuples)
            if content_tuples:
                execute_values(cur, """INSERT INTO content (page_id, cleaned_text) VALUES %s ON CONFLICT (page_id) DO NOTHING;""", content_tuples)
            conn.commit()
        except Exception as e:
            conn.rollback()
            raise e
        finally:
            cur.close()
            if own_connection:
                conn.close()
    
    def load_ingest_progress(manifest_id):
        """Load byte-range checkpoints: {file_url: (bytes_done, articles_done, complete)}."""
        conn = get_connection()
        try:
            cur = conn.cursor()
            try:
                cur.execute(
                    "SELECT file_url, bytes_done, articles_done, complete "
                    "FROM ingest_files WHERE manifest_id = %s;",
                    (manifest_id,),
                )
                return {row[0]: (row[1], row[2], row[3]) for row in cur.fetchall()}
            finally:
                cur.close()
        finally:
            conn.close()
    
    
    def upsert_ingest_progress(manifest_id, file_url, bytes_done, articles_done, complete):
        """Store a checkpoint. Counters are monotonic; complete is sticky."""
        conn = get_connection()
        try:
            cur = conn.cursor()
            try:
                cur.execute(
                    """
                    INSERT INTO ingest_files
                        (file_url, manifest_id, bytes_done, articles_done, complete, updated_at)
                    VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)
                    ON CONFLICT (file_url) DO UPDATE SET
                        bytes_done = GREATEST(ingest_files.bytes_done, EXCLUDED.bytes_done),
                        articles_done = GREATEST(ingest_files.articles_done, EXCLUDED.articles_done),
                        complete = ingest_files.complete OR EXCLUDED.complete,
                        updated_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP;
                    """,
                    (file_url, manifest_id, bytes_done, articles_done, complete),
                )
                conn.commit()
            finally:
                cur.close()
        finally:
            conn.close()
    
    
    if __name__ == "__main__":
        init_db()
    import re
    from typing import Optional, Tuple
    from charset_normalizer import from_bytes
    
    ARABIC_CHAR_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\u0600-\u06FF\u0750-\u077F\u08A0-\u08FF]')
    # Letters that never occur in genuine Arabic but are routine in neighbours
    # sharing the script. Lingua lacks Kurdish, so Sorani markers are the
    # backstop there; fa/ur markers catch Persian/Urdu that still scores 'ar'.
    NON_ARABIC_MARKERS = re.compile(
        '['
        '\u067E\u0686\u0698\u06AF\u06A9\u06CC'  # fa: pe che jeem gaf kaf yeh
        '\u0679\u0688\u0691\u06BA\u06BE\u06D2'  # ur: tte ddahal rre noon-ghunna he-do-chashmee bare-ye
        '\u06D5\u0695\u06B5\u06C6\u06CE'        # ku (Sorani): ae rra lla o u
        ']'
    )
    
    def is_target_language(lang_code: str) -> bool:
        if not lang_code:
            return False
        lang = str(lang_code).lower().strip()
        return lang in {"ar", "en", "fr"}
    
    def is_clean_encoding(text: str) -> bool:
        if not text or "\ufffd" in text:
            return False
        mojibake_chars = sum(1 for c in text if c in '¤§¼«¬¦¶±µ°¥¢£©®™¿ÀÁÂÃÄÅÆÇÈÉÊËÌÍÎÏÐÑÒÓÔÕÖØÙÚÛÜÝÞßàáâãäåæçèéêëìíîïðñòóôõöøùúûüýþÿ')
        if len(text) > 0 and (mojibake_chars / len(text)) > 0.03:
            return False
        return True
    
    def is_valid_arabic_text(text: str) -> bool:
        if not text or len(text) < 20:
            return False
        sample = text[:500]
        arabic_chars = len(ARABIC_CHAR_PATTERN.findall(sample))
        alpha_chars = sum(1 for c in sample if c.isalpha())
        if alpha_chars == 0:
            return False
        if (arabic_chars / alpha_chars) < 0.40:
            return False
        # Arabic-script neighbours (fa/ur/ku): a handful of distinctive letters
        # disqualifies, genuine Arabic carries none of them.
        markers = len(NON_ARABIC_MARKERS.findall(sample))
        if markers >= 2 and (markers / max(arabic_chars, 1)) > 0.02:
            return False
        return True
    
    def decode_and_validate(raw_bytes: bytes) -> Tuple[Optional[str], str]:
        if not raw_bytes:
            return None, "empty_bytes"
        decoded_text = None
        try:
            decoded_text = raw_bytes.decode("utf-8", errors="strict")
        except UnicodeDecodeError:
            pass
        if not decoded_text:
            try:
                results = from_bytes(raw_bytes)
                best_match = results.best()
                if best_match and best_match.encoding:
                    if not (best_match.encoding.lower() in ["iso-8859-1", "latin-1"] and best_match.coherence < 0.8):
                        decoded_text = str(best_match)
            except Exception:
                pass
        if not decoded_text:
            try:
                decoded_text = raw_bytes.decode("windows-1256", errors="strict")
            except UnicodeDecodeError:
                pass
        if not decoded_text:
            return None, "decode_failed"
        if not is_clean_encoding(decoded_text):
            return None, "encoding_corrupted"
        return decoded_text, "success"

    import os
    import re
    import json
    from typing import Dict, Any, List
    from selectolax.parser import HTMLParser
    from utils import decode_and_validate, is_valid_arabic_text
    
    ARABIC_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\u0600-\u06FF]')
    LATIN_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[a-zA-Z]')
    CYRILLIC_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\u0400-\u04FF]')
    # CJK scripts: kana is Japanese-specific, hangul Korean-specific, han shared
    # (mostly Chinese in news, also used in Japanese).
    KANA_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\u3040-\u30FF]')
    HANGUL_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\uAC00-\uD7AF\u1100-\u11FF]')
    CJK_PATTERN = re.compile(r'[\u3040-\u30FF\u4E00-\u9FFF\uAC00-\uD7AF]')
    
    USE_TRAFILATURA = os.environ.get("USE_TRAFILATURA", "1") == "1"
    TARGET_LANGUAGES = {"ar", "en", "fr"}
    
    UNWANTED_TAGS = ["script", "style", "noscript", "header", "footer", "svg", "nav"]
    
    # Language ID via Lingua (n-gram models, no torch). Detector is built lazily
    # once per process; workers never call it (detection runs driver-side only).
    LINGUA_LANGUAGES = (
        "ARABIC", "ENGLISH", "FRENCH", "SPANISH", "GERMAN", "ITALIAN",
        "PORTUGUESE", "CATALAN", "ROMANIAN", "RUSSIAN", "TURKISH",
        "VIETNAMESE", "JAPANESE", "KOREAN", "CHINESE", "DUTCH", "TAMIL",
        "UKRAINIAN", "PERSIAN", "URDU",
    )
    LINGUA_MIN_CONFIDENCE = float(os.environ.get("LINGUA_MIN_CONFIDENCE", "0.5"))
    _lingua_detector = None
    
    
    def get_lingua_detector():
        global _lingua_detector
        if _lingua_detector is None:
            from lingua import Language, LanguageDetectorBuilder
            _lingua_detector = (
                LanguageDetectorBuilder.from_languages(
                    *[getattr(Language, name) for name in LINGUA_LANGUAGES]
                )
                .with_preloaded_language_models()
                .build()
            )
        return _lingua_detector
    
    
    _LINGUA_TO_CODE = {
        "ARABIC": "ar",
        "ENGLISH": "en",
        "FRENCH": "fr",
        "RUSSIAN": "ru",
        "JAPANESE": "ja",
        "KOREAN": "ko",
        "CHINESE": "zh",
        "PERSIAN": "fa",
        "URDU": "ur",
    }
    
    # Full Latin block (ASCII patterns miss French accents). Used only by the
    # script gate below; existing patterns stay untouched.
    _LATIN_FULL_PATTERN = re.compile(r"[a-zA-ZÀ-ɏḀ-ỿ]")
    _SUPPORTED_SCRIPTS = re.compile(
        r"[a-zA-ZÀ-ɏḀ-ỿ\u0600-\u06FF\u0750-\u077F\u08A0-\u08FF\u0400-\u04FF"
        r"\u3040-\u30FF\u4E00-\u9FFF\uAC00-\uD7AF\u1100-\u11FF]"
    )
    
    
    def _unsupported_script_share(text: str) -> float:
        """Share of alpha chars outside Latin/Arabic/Cyrillic/CJK.
    
        Catches bodies in unloaded scripts (Kannada, Hindi, Bengali, ...) that
        boilerplate chrome smuggled past lingua's head sample: the chrome votes
        Latin, but a fifth or more of the full text is none of the above.
        """
        if not text:
            return 1.0
        alpha = [c for c in text if c.isalpha()]
        if not alpha:
            return 1.0
        other = sum(1 for c in alpha if not _SUPPORTED_SCRIPTS.match(c))
        return other / len(alpha)
    
    
    def detect_languages_batch(texts: List[str]) -> List[str]:
        if not texts:
            return []
        detector = get_lingua_detector()
        results = []
        for text in texts:
            if not text or len(text) < 20:
                results.append("unknown")
                continue
            sample = text[:2000]
            has_script = (
                ARABIC_PATTERN.search(sample)
                or LATIN_PATTERN.search(sample)
                or CYRILLIC_PATTERN.search(sample)
                or CJK_PATTERN.search(sample)
            )
            if not has_script:
                results.append("ignored")
                continue
            try:
                confidence = detector.compute_language_confidence_values(sample)
            except Exception:
                results.append("other")
                continue
            if not confidence or confidence[0].value <= 0.0:
                results.append("ignored")
                continue
            top = confidence[0]
            if top.value < LINGUA_MIN_CONFIDENCE:
                results.append("other")
                continue
            code = _LINGUA_TO_CODE.get(top.language.name, "other")
            if code in ("en", "fr", "ar") and _unsupported_script_share(text) > 0.10:
                results.append("other")
                continue
            if code == "ar" and not is_valid_arabic_text(text):
                results.append("corrupted")
                continue
            results.append(code)
        return results
    
    def extract_publication_date(tree: HTMLParser) -> str:
        meta_selectors = [
            ("meta[property='article:published_time']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='pubdate']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='publishdate']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='date']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='DC.date.issued']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='parsely-pub-date']", "content")
        ]
        for selector, attr in meta_selectors:
            node = tree.css_first(selector)
            if node and node.attributes.get(attr):
                date_val = node.attributes.get(attr).strip()
                if date_val:
                    return date_val
        for script_node in tree.css("script[type='application/ld+json']"):
            raw = script_node.text(strip=True)
            if raw:
                try:
                    data = json.loads(raw)
                    candidates = data if isinstance(data, list) else [data]
                    for item in candidates:
                        if isinstance(item, dict):
                            date_val = item.get("datePublished") or item.get("dateCreated")
                            if date_val:
                                return str(date_val).strip()
                except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
                    continue
        time_node = tree.css_first("time")
        if time_node:
            datetime_val = time_node.attributes.get("datetime")
            if datetime_val:
                return datetime_val.strip()
            text_val = time_node.text(strip=True)
            if text_val:
                return text_val
        return "N/A"
    
    def extract_date_from_text_fallback(tree: HTMLParser) -> str:
        body_text = tree.body.text() if tree.body else tree.text()
        date_match = re.search(r'\b(19\d\d|20\d\d)[-/.](0?[1-9]|1[0-2])[-/.](0?[1-9]|[12]\d|3[01])\b', body_text)
        return date_match.group(0) if date_match else "N/A"
    
    def extract_author(tree: HTMLParser) -> str:
        author_selectors = [
            ("meta[name='author']", "content"),
            ("meta[property='article:author']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='parsely-author']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='dc.creator']", "content"),
            ("meta[name='author_name']", "content")
        ]
        for selector, attr in author_selectors:
            node = tree.css_first(selector)
            if node and node.attributes.get(attr):
                val = node.attributes.get(attr).strip()
                if val:
                    return val
        for script_node in tree.css("script[type='application/ld+json']"):
            raw = script_node.text(strip=True)
            if raw:
                try:
                    data = json.loads(raw)
                    candidates = data if isinstance(data, list) else [data]
                    for item in candidates:
                        if isinstance(item, dict) and "author" in item:
                            author_data = item["author"]
                            if isinstance(author_data, dict) and "name" in author_data:
                                return str(author_data["name"]).strip()
                            elif isinstance(author_data, list) and len(author_data) > 0:
                                first_author = author_data[0]
                                if isinstance(first_author, dict):
                                    return str(first_author.get("name", "N/A")).strip()
                                return str(first_author).strip()
                            elif isinstance(author_data, str):
                                return author_data.strip()
                except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
                    continue
        return "N/A"
    
    def _trafilatura_text(html_str: str) -> str:
        """Main-content extraction via trafilatura. Returns '' on failure/None."""
        try:
            from trafilatura import extract as _trafi_extract
            text = _trafi_extract(html_str, include_comments=False, include_tables=False)
        except Exception:
            return ""
        if not text:
            return ""
        return " ".join(text.split())
    
    
    def extract_html_fields(record: Dict[str, Any], min_words: int = 80) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        html_str, status = decode_and_validate(record["raw_bytes"])
        if status != "success" or not html_str:
            return {"language": "corrupted", "word_count": 0, "clean_text": "", "extraction": "corrupted"}
        tree = HTMLParser(html_str)
        title_node = tree.css_first("title")
        title = title_node.text(strip=True) if title_node else "N/A"
        for tag in tree.css(", ".join(UNWANTED_TAGS)):
            tag.decompose()
        body = tree.body
        raw_text = body.text(separator=' ', strip=True) if body else tree.text(separator=' ', strip=True)
        fallback_text = " ".join(raw_text.split())
        clean_text = fallback_text
        extraction = "selectolax"
        if USE_TRAFILATURA:
            trafi_text = _trafilatura_text(html_str)
            if trafi_text and len(trafi_text.split()) >= min_words:
                clean_text = trafi_text
                extraction = "trafilatura"
        word_cnt = len(clean_text.split())
        if word_cnt < min_words:
            return {"language": "too_short", "word_count": word_cnt, "clean_text": "", "extraction": extraction}
        pub_date = extract_publication_date(tree)
        if pub_date == "N/A":
            pub_date = extract_date_from_text_fallback(tree)
        author = extract_author(tree)
        return {
            "title": title,
            "author": author,
            "url": record["url"],
            "warc_date": record["warc_date"],
            "published_date": pub_date,
            "clean_text": clean_text,
            "word_count": word_cnt,
            "char_count": len(clean_text),
            "html_size_bytes": len(record["raw_bytes"]),
            "links_count": len(tree.css("a")),
            "headings_sample": [node.text(strip=True) for node in tree.css("h1, h2, h3")][:3],
            "extraction": extraction,
        }#!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    
    PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
    cd "$PROJECT_ROOT"
    
    TMPDIR="$(mktemp -d)"
    ZIP="$TMPDIR/project_code.zip"
    VENV_ARCHIVE="$PROJECT_ROOT/pyspark_venv.tar.gz"
    
    trap 'rm -rf "$TMPDIR"' EXIT
    
    # Activate local driver environment
    source "$PROJECT_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate"
    
    # Build the packed venv only if it does not already exist
    if [ ! -f "$VENV_ARCHIVE" ]; then
        echo "Building PySpark worker environment..."
        venv-pack -o "$VENV_ARCHIVE"
    fi
    
    # Package current project code on every run
    echo "Packaging project code..."
    zip -qr "$ZIP" \
        extractors \
        parsers \
        db \
        utils.py
    
    # Driver uses the local venv on spark-master
    export PYSPARK_DRIVER_PYTHON="$PROJECT_ROOT/.venv/bin/python"
    
    # Executors use the environment distributed by YARN
    export PYSPARK_PYTHON="./environment/bin/python"
    
    # Tunables (override per run, e.g. NUM_FILES=2 ./run_to_db.sh)
    #   NUM_FILES: files to process from START_FILE_OFFSET; unset/empty/all/0 = to end of manifest
    #   START_FILE_OFFSET: skip the first N manifest entries (file-range targeting); 0 = from start
    #   MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND: per-round transfer chunk (extracted articles); 0 = unlimited
    #   MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE: per-file stop on accepted (target-language + fresh) articles; 0 = file runs to EOF
    #   MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND: in-flight files per round (bounds task results); 0 = all unfinished
    #   MIN_WORD_COUNT: article = this many words+ after extraction (trafilatura acceptance + yield gate)
    #   MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES: global stop on accepted articles; 0 = unlimited (get-all)
    #   USE_HDFS_CACHE: 0 disables both shared HDFS artifacts (worker venv + Spark jars)
    export NUM_FILES="${NUM_FILES:-15}"
    export START_FILE_OFFSET="${START_FILE_OFFSET:-0}"
    export MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND="${MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND:-2000}"
    export MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES="${MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES:-0}"
    # Per-file accepted cap (driver-side rounds); 0 = each file runs to EOF.
    export MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE="${MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE:-0}"
    # Files per round (in-flight partitions); 0 = all unfinished files each round.
    export MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND="${MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND:-6}"
    # Article = MIN_WORD_COUNT+ words after extraction.
    export MIN_WORD_COUNT="${MIN_WORD_COUNT:-80}"
    
    # --- Shared venv on HDFS (upload once, reuse across runs) ---
    # Per-run --archives of the worker venv re-uploads + re-localizes on every
    # submit and fills NM disks. Instead we store a content-addressed copy on
    # HDFS and reference it; rebuilds (new md5) upload exactly once, old copies
    # are pruned.
    HDFS_APPS_DIR="${HDFS_APPS_DIR:-/user/spark-master/apps/spark}"
    VENV_MD5="$(md5sum "$VENV_ARCHIVE" | awk '{print $1}')"
    HDFS_VENV="$HDFS_APPS_DIR/pyspark_venv-${VENV_MD5}.tar.gz"
    ARCHIVES_SPEC="$VENV_ARCHIVE#environment"   # fallback: local per-run upload
    
    hdfs_ok() { hdfs dfs -test -e / >/dev/null 2>&1; }
    
    upload_venv_hdfs() {
        # Best-effort: any failure returns non-zero so the caller falls back
        # to a local per-run upload instead of aborting the submit (set -e).
        hdfs dfs -mkdir -p "$HDFS_APPS_DIR" 2>/dev/null || return 1
        hdfs dfs -put -f "$VENV_ARCHIVE" "$HDFS_VENV" 2>/dev/null || return 1
        # Prune older venv builds, keep newest 2
        hdfs dfs -ls "$HDFS_APPS_DIR"/pyspark_venv-'*.tar.gz' 2>/dev/null \
            | sort -k6,7 \
            | head -n -2 \
            | awk '{print $8}' \
            | xargs -r hdfs dfs -rm -skipTrash >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
        return 0
    }
    
    if [ "${USE_HDFS_CACHE:-1}" = "1" ] && hdfs_ok; then
        if hdfs dfs -test -e "$HDFS_VENV" 2>/dev/null; then
            echo "Reusing HDFS venv $HDFS_VENV (no upload needed)."
            ARCHIVES_SPEC="hdfs://$HDFS_VENV#environment"
        elif upload_venv_hdfs; then
            echo "Uploaded venv to HDFS ($HDFS_VENV, one-time for this build)."
            ARCHIVES_SPEC="hdfs://$HDFS_VENV#environment"
        else
            echo "WARNING: HDFS venv upload failed — falling back to per-run venv upload."
        fi
    else
        echo "WARNING: HDFS unreachable or USE_HDFS_CACHE=0 — falling back to per-run venv upload."
    fi
    
    # --- Shared Spark jars archive (default on, verified 2026-09-28) ---
    # Avoids the per-run "falling back to uploading libraries under SPARK_HOME".
    # Disable per-run with USE_HDFS_CACHE=0.
    EXTRA_CONF=()
    if [ "${USE_HDFS_CACHE:-1}" = "1" ] && hdfs_ok; then
        SPARK_JARS_ZIP="$PROJECT_ROOT/spark-libs.zip"
        HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE="$HDFS_APPS_DIR/spark-libs.zip"
        if [ ! -f "$SPARK_JARS_ZIP" ]; then
            echo "Building Spark jars archive (one-time)..."
            (cd "${SPARK_HOME:?SPARK_HOME not set}/jars" && zip -qr "$SPARK_JARS_ZIP" .)
        fi
        if hdfs dfs -test -e "$HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE" 2>/dev/null; then
            echo "Reusing HDFS Spark archive $HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE."
            EXTRA_CONF+=(--conf "spark.yarn.archive=hdfs://$HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE")
        elif hdfs dfs -mkdir -p "$HDFS_APPS_DIR" 2>/dev/null \
            && hdfs dfs -put -f "$SPARK_JARS_ZIP" "$HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE" 2>/dev/null; then
            echo "Uploaded Spark jars archive to HDFS (one-time)."
            EXTRA_CONF+=(--conf "spark.yarn.archive=hdfs://$HDFS_SPARK_ARCHIVE")
        else
            echo "WARNING: Spark archive upload failed — continuing without spark.yarn.archive."
        fi
    fi
    
    echo "Submitting stream_to_db job to YARN (NUM_FILES=$NUM_FILES from offset $START_FILE_OFFSET, MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND=$MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND, MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE=$MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE, MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND=$MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND, MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES=$MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES)..."
    
    spark-submit \
        --master yarn \
        --deploy-mode client \
        --conf "spark.pyspark.driver.python=$PROJECT_ROOT/.venv/bin/python" \
        --conf "spark.pyspark.python=./environment/bin/python" \
        "${EXTRA_CONF[@]}" \
        --archives "$ARCHIVES_SPEC" \
        --py-files "$ZIP" \
        stream_to_db.py

        28    # Resume overlap: rewind this far before the stored byte offset so the
        29    # resync scan lands on a valid gzip member. Overlap duplicates collapse
        30    # driver-side via record_id (upsert-safe); the straddled boundary article
        31    # is the accepted loss. 0 disables byte resume (full GET every file).
        32    RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("RESUME_OVERLAP_BYTES", str(1024 * 1024)))
        33    GZIP_MAGIC = b"\x1f\x8b"
        34
        35
        36    class _CountingReader:
        37        """File-like byte counter for worker-side resume offsets.
        38
        39        Wraps the HTTP response; warcio reads through it while every byte is
        40        counted. A pushback buffer lets the gzip resync scan return the member
        41        header it stopped at.
        42        """
        43
        44        def __init__(self, raw):
        45            self._raw = raw
        46            self._buf = b""
        47            self.bytes_read = 0
        48
        49        def read(self, size=-1):
        50            if size is None or size < 0:
        51                chunks = [self._buf]
        52                self._buf = b""
        53                while True:
        54                    data = self._raw.read(65536)
        55                    if not data:
        56                        break
        57                    chunks.append(data)
        58                out = b"".join(chunks)
        59            else:
        60                while len(self._buf) < size:
        61                    data = self._raw.read(max(65536, size - len(self._buf)))
        62                    if not data:
        63                        break
        64                    self._buf += data
        65                out, self._buf = self._buf[:size], self._buf[size:]
        66            self.bytes_read += len(out)
        67            return out
        68
        69        def pushback(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        70            self._buf = data + self._buf
        71            self.bytes_read -= len(data)
        72
        73        def __getattr__(self, name):
        74            # Delegate anything else (close, seekable, ...) to the raw stream
        75            # so warcio sees the file-like it expects.
        76            return getattr(self.__dict__["_raw"], name)
        77
        78
        79    def _resync_gzip_member(reader: _CountingReader, max_scan: int = 8 * 1024 * 1024) -> bool:
        80        """Advance a Range-resumed stream to the next gzip member boundary.
        81
        82        Returns True once the member header is pushed back for the parser.
        83        False when no valid boundary appears within max_scan (caller falls
        84        back to a full GET). Header check is magic + CM==deflate + reserved
        85        FLG bits clear; false positives are ~1e-8 per position.
        86        """
        87        window = b""
        88        scanned = 0
        89        while scanned < max_scan:
        90            chunk = reader.read(65536)
        91            if not chunk:
        92                return False
        93            window += chunk
        94            scanned += len(chunk)
        95            pos = 0
        96            while True:
        97                idx = window.find(GZIP_MAGIC, pos)
        98                if idx == -1:
        99                    window = window[-1:]  # keep tail split-magic bytes
       100                    break
       101                if idx + 4 > len(window):
       102                    window = window[idx:]  # need more bytes to validate
       103                    pos = 0
       104                    break
       105                cm, flg = window[idx + 2], window[idx + 3]
       106                if cm == 8 and (flg & 0xE0) == 0:
       107                    reader.pushback(window[idx:])
       108                    return True
       109                pos = idx + 1
       110        return False
       111
       112
       113    PATHS_URL = f"https://data.commoncrawl.org/crawl-data/CC-NEWS/{YEAR}/{MONTH}/warc.paths.gz"
       114    HEADERS = {"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64)"}
       115    DB_BATCH_SIZE = int(os.environ.get("DB_BATCH_SIZE", "25"))
       116
       117    MAX_RETRIES = int(os.environ.get("MAX_RETRIES", "3"))
       118    DEAD_LETTER_DIR = os.environ.get("DEAD_LETTER_DIR", os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "dead_letter"))
       119
       120    # Article caps. MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND <= 0 means unlimited transfer per
       121    # round (worker-visible extracted count, the per-round transfer chunk).
       122    # MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES <= 0 means unlimited accepted articles for the run.
       123    # NUM_FILES None means to the end of the manifest ("end of the paths file").
       124    # START_FILE_OFFSET skips the first N manifest entries (file-range targeting).
       125    MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND = int(os.environ.get("MAX_EXTRACTED_PER_ROUND", "2000"))
       126    # Per-file accepted cap (target-language + fresh, counted driver-side).
       127    # The worker can't know acceptance (HF runs on the driver), so each file is
       128    # processed in rounds: every round resumes from the stored byte checkpoint
       129    # and the driver stops re-submitting the file once its accepted count hits
       130    # this cap (0 = unlimited, file runs to EOF).
       131    MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE = int(os.environ.get("MAX_ACCEPTED_PER_FILE", "0"))
       132    # Files per round: bounds in-flight partitions/task-results no matter how big
       133    # the manifest is (0 = all unfinished files every round). Default matches the
       134    # executor count so each executor typically hosts one file-task.
       135    MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND = int(os.environ.get("MAX_FILES_PER_ROUND", "6"))
       136    # Article = MIN_WORD_COUNT+ words after extraction (both the trafilatura
       137    # acceptance and the yield gate; passed to workers via task closure).
       138    MIN_WORD_COUNT = int(os.environ.get("MIN_WORD_COUNT", "80"))
       139    MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES = int(os.environ.get("MAX_ACCEPTED_ARTICLES", "0"))
       140    # Driver progress bar (stderr, silent when piped). NO_PROGRESS=1 disables it.
       141    USE_PROGRESS_BAR = os.environ.get("NO_PROGRESS", "0") != "1"
       142

       144    def _parse_num_files(raw: str | None) -> int | None:
       145        if raw is None:
       146            return 15
       147        text = raw.strip().lower()
       148        if text in ("", "all", "none"):
       149            return None
       150        try:
       151            value = int(text)
       152        except ValueError:
       153            return 15
       154        return None if value <= 0 else value
       155
       156
       157    NUM_FILES = _parse_num_files(os.environ.get("NUM_FILES"))
       158    START_FILE_OFFSET = max(0, int(os.environ.get("START_FILE_OFFSET", "0") or 0))

       161    def get_spark_session() -> SparkSession:
       162        try:
       163            existing_session = SparkSession.getActiveSession()
       164            if existing_session:
       165                return existing_session
       166        except Exception:
       167            pass
       168
       169        builder = (
       170            SparkSession.builder
       171            .appName("CC-NEWS-Stream-To-DB-Distributed-Cluster")
       172            # Inherit resource limits from /opt/spark/conf/spark-defaults.conf
       173            # or spark-submit; this cluster has three workers with 2 GB RAM each.
       174            .config("spark.sql.execution.arrow.pyspark.enabled", "true")
       175            # Task results under this size ride back on the existing
       176            # executor->driver channel (direct). Larger ones are stored in the
       177            # executor BlockManager and fetched over a NEW driver->executor
       178            # connection, which this cluster refuses: keep results direct.
       179            .config("spark.task.maxDirectResultSize", "32m")
       180        )
       181        # Only force a master when explicitly requested. Otherwise inherit it
       182        # from spark-submit (--master yarn) so the job actually uses workers.
       183        # Direct Python invocation also loads this installation's Spark defaults.
       184        if "SPARK_MASTER_URL" in os.environ:
       185            builder = builder.master(os.environ["SPARK_MASTER_URL"])
       186        return builder.getOrCreate()
       187

       189    def make_record_id(record, url, text):
       190        rid = record.get("record_id", "")
       191        if rid:
       192            return rid
       193        basis = (url or "") + (text[:200] if text else "")
       194        return "gen-" + hashlib.md5(basis.encode("utf-8", errors="ignore")).hexdigest()
       195
       196
       197    def _is_transient_download_error(error: Exception) -> bool:
       198        if isinstance(error, (socket.timeout, TimeoutError, ConnectionError)):
       199            return True
       200        if isinstance(error, urllib.error.HTTPError):
       201            return error.code == 429 or 500 <= error.code < 600
       202        if isinstance(error, urllib.error.URLError):
       203            return True
       204        return False
       205
       206
       207    def _sleep_backoff(attempt: int) -> None:
       208        time.sleep(2 ** (attempt + 1))
       209
       210
       211    def _write_dead_letter(batch: list, error: Exception) -> str:
       212        os.makedirs(DEAD_LETTER_DIR, exist_ok=True)
       213        stamp = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ")
       214        path = os.path.join(DEAD_LETTER_DIR, f"failed-batch-{stamp}-{os.getpid()}.jsonl")
       215        payload = {"error": f"{type(error).__name__}: {error}", "records": batch}
       216        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
       217            json.dump(payload, fh, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)
       218            fh.write("\n")
       219        return path
       220
       221
       222    def _filter_already_saved(conn, record_ids: list) -> set:
       223        """Return the subset of record_ids already present in pages."""
       224        if not record_ids:
       225            return set()
       226        cur = conn.cursor()
       227        try:
       228            cur.execute(
       229                "SELECT warc_record_id FROM pages WHERE warc_record_id = ANY(%s);",
       230                (record_ids,),
       231            )
       232            return {row[0] for row in cur.fetchall()}
       233        finally:
       234            cur.close()
       235
       236
       237    def _save_batch_with_retry(batch: list) -> tuple[int, str | None]:
       238        """Save one batch with reconnect + backoff. Returns (saved_count, dead_letter_path)."""
       239        last_error: Exception | None = None
       240        for attempt in range(MAX_RETRIES):
       241            conn = None
       242            try:
       243                conn = get_connection()
       244                saved_before = len(batch)
       245                save_batch_records(batch, conn=conn)
       246                return saved_before, None
       247            except Exception as error:
       248                last_error = error
       249                try:
       250                    if conn is not None:
       251                        conn.rollback()
       252                except Exception:
       253                    pass
       254                print(f"DB batch write failed (attempt {attempt + 1}/{MAX_RETRIES}): {error}")
       255                if attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1:
       256                    _sleep_backoff(attempt)
       257            finally:
       258                try:
       259                    if conn is not None:
       260                        conn.close()
       261                except Exception:
       262                    pass
       263        assert last_error is not None
       264        path = _write_dead_letter(batch, last_error)
       265        print(f"DB batch written to dead-letter file, continuing: {path}")
       266        return 0, path
       267
       268
       269    def _flush_batch(db_batch: list, seen_ids: set, stats: dict, file_accepted: dict) -> list:
       270        """Language-filter + resume-filter + save one accumulated batch. Returns []."""
       271        if not db_batch:
       272            return []
       273        conn = None
       274        try:
       275            conn = get_connection()
       276            already = _filter_already_saved(conn, [item["record_id"] for item in db_batch])
       277        except Exception as error:
       278            print(f"Resume pre-check failed, proceeding to upsert anyway: {error}")
       279            already = set()
       280        finally:
       281            try:
       282                if conn is not None:
       283                    conn.close()
       284            except Exception:
       285                pass

         2    from typing import Generator, Dict, Any, Optional

         5    def _readable_stream(value):
         6        if isinstance(value, (bytes, bytearray)):
         7            return io.BytesIO(value)
         8        if hasattr(value, "read"):
         9            return value
        10        raise TypeError(f"Unsupported WARC input type: {type(value)!r}")

        12
        13    def parse_warc_records_streaming(stream) -> Generator[Dict[str, Any], None, None]:
        14        """Record-at-a-time streaming WARC parser.
        15
        16        Parses each response record as its bytes arrive over a non-seekable
        17        stream (e.g. an open HTTP response). Holds O(one article) in memory and
        18        O(1) total — the file is never staged to disk or RAM. A truncated tail
        19        (connection close, early abort) ends iteration silently; complete records
        20        yielded so far are unaffected.
        21        """
        22        from warcio.archiveiterator import ArchiveIterator
        23
        24        stream = _readable_stream(stream)
        25        try:
        26            iterator = ArchiveIterator(stream, arc2warc=True)
        27            for record in iterator:
        28                try:
        29                    if record.rec_type != "response":
        30                        continue
        31                    content_type = ""
        32                    if record.http_headers:
        33                        try:
        34                            content_type = record.http_headers.get_header("Content-Type") or ""
        35                        except Exception:
        36                            content_type = ""
        37                    if "text/html" not in content_type.lower():
        38                        continue
        39                    try:
        40                        raw_bytes = record.content_stream().read()
        41                    except Exception:
        42                        continue
        43                    if not raw_bytes:
        44                        continue
        45                    headers = record.rec_headers
        46                    yield {
        47                        "url": headers.get_header("WARC-Target-URI"),
        48                        "warc_date": headers.get_header("WARC-Date"),
        49                        "content_type": content_type,
        50                        "record_id": headers.get_header("WARC-Record-ID"),
        51                        "raw_bytes": raw_bytes,
        52                    }
        53                except Exception:
        54                    continue
        55        except Exception:
        56            # Truncated stream tail (early close, cut connection): stop iteration,
        57            # keep the complete records already yielded.
        58            return
        59
        60
        61    def parse_warc_stream_fastwarc(stream, max_warc_bytes: Optional[int] = None, **kwargs) -> Generator[Dict[str, Any], None, None]:
        62        """Streaming entry point (name kept for backwards compatibility).
        63
        64        Uses the warcio record-at-a-time path: no byte cap, no temp file.
        65        Extra args (e.g. legacy max_warc_bytes) are accepted and ignored.
        66        """
        67        _ = max_warc_bytes
        68        _ = kwargs
        69        yield from parse_warc_records_streaming(stream)

       511                        # known end without a cap break or an error. Unknown size
       512                        # keeps the legacy silent-end semantics.
       513                        consumed = wire_base + counter.bytes_read
       514                        eof = not capped and (
       515                            total_size is None or consumed >= total_size - 1
       516                        )
       517                        yield {
       518                            "__file_done__": True,
       519                            "file_url": file_url,
       520                            "file_bytes": consumed,
       521                            "eof": eof,
       522                        }
       523                        break
       524                    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
       525                        if error.code == 416 and not range_failed:
       526                            # Stored offset past EOF (file shrank / overshoot):
       527                            # retry once as a full GET.
       528                            print(f"Range unsatisfiable for {file_url}, falling back to full GET.")
       529                            range_failed = True
       530                            try:
       531                                if response is not None:
       532                                    response.close()
       533                            except Exception:
       534                                pass
       535                            continue
       536                        print(f"Download/parse of {file_url} failed (attempt {attempt + 1}/{MAX_RETRIES}): {error}")
       537                        if attempt >= MAX_RETRIES - 1 or not _is_transient_download_error(error):
       538                            print(f"SKIP {file_url}: {error}")
       539                            break
       540                        _sleep_backoff(attempt)
       541                    except Exception as error:
       542                        print(f"Download/parse of {file_url} failed (attempt {attempt + 1}/{MAX_RETRIES}): {error}")
       543                        if attempt >= MAX_RETRIES - 1 or not _is_transient_download_error(error):
       544                            print(f"SKIP {file_url}: {error}")
       545                            break
       546                        _sleep_backoff(attempt)
       547                    finally:
       548                        try:
       549                            if response is not None:
       550                                response.close()
       551                        except Exception:
       552                            pass
       553
       554        print(
       555            f"Starting Streaming Pipeline into PostgreSQL | files={len(warc_urls)} "
       556            f"chunk_per_round={'unlimited' if chunk_cap is None else chunk_cap} (extracted) "

       610        def _consume(record_iter) -> None:
       611            """Drain one round's iterator: batch, flush, checkpoint, bar."""
       612            nonlocal db_batch, limit_reached
       613            try:
       614                for record in record_iter:

       616                        # File boundary: flush first, then commit (crash-safe order).

       620                            _commit_progress()
       621                            _refresh_bar()
       622                        if furl:
       623                            _commit_progress({furl: bool(record.get("eof", False))})
       624                            _refresh_bar()
       625                        continue
